#6765. Dreaming of Buying a Home Abroad? These 10 Countries Make It Shockingly Simple

If you’ve always wanted to live out your expat fantasies, start here.

Over the last few years, many Americans have daydreamed about life abroad. Moving internationally can often feel like a pipe dream, but with the right planning, it’s possible. However, lengthy visa processes, daunting language barriers, and a difficult-to-navigate legal system can make the dream feel more like a nightmare.

Certain countries, like Australia, Canada, and New Zealand, have bans on foreign buyers. Others, like Thailand, Cuba, and Vietnam, don’t allow outsiders to own property at all. On the other end of the spectrum are countries actively trying to attract foreign buyers, offering enviable incentives like affordable home prices, a smoother customs process, and an easier-to-navigate work-visa approval process.

Choosing the right location can make moving abroad much less intimidating. Ahead, we’re breaking down the ten best countries for Americans looking to buy property abroad. Whether you’re dreaming of relocating or searching for your perfect vacation home, these countries make buying as easy as possible and offer gorgeous landscapes too.

If you’ve ever dreamed of living among medieval architecture and rolling mountain ranges, Georgia might be the key to unlocking your fantasy. A bridge between Eastern Europe and Western Asia, the transcontinental country borders Russia, Turkey, Armenia, and the Black Sea, offering both subtropical and high-altitude climates. Thankfully, the real estate landscape is much less complex, with few legal or bureaucratic hurdles for American buyers, no residency or visa required (for up to a year), and remarkably fast processing times. In fact, the only major restriction in the Georgia market is that foreigners cannot purchase or own agricultural land, a nod to the importance of the local, small-scale farming cultures peppered across the country’s regions.

Uruguay’s buying process could not be more straightforward. South America’s second-smallest sovereign country requires no citizenship, visa, or prior approvals. Under Uruguayan law, foreign buyers are given the same rights as citizens and enjoy the benefits of free capital flow. Locals are just as, if not more, tourist-friendly. The country is known for its hospitality, laid-back café culture, and stunning coastal landscapes.

Instead of retiring to Florida, consider this balmy, beautiful Caribbean country—just a four-hour direct flight from the New York area. Like everything else on the island, the real estate laws are relatively relaxed, making buying a house easy-breezy; and you can buy and own property without obtaining residency status or special permissions, as is the case in many other Caribbean islands. “Foreigners can own property outright in the Dominican Republic, with legal protections for foreign investors,” says Mauricio Umansky, CEO and founder of The Agency. One important consideration is financing: You can certainly get a mortgage in the DR, but it may not be quite as cushy as what you can get in the U.S.—so pay cash if possible.

Great news if you’ve been wanting a more provincial life: Americans face zero restrictions when buying homes in France. The open market allows for statesiders to buy houses, apartments, or plots of land in the city, countryside, and coastal towns in person or remotely without needing citizenship, residency, or even a local visa. However, a purchase does not grant residency, so if you’re hoping to make France your permanent home, you’ll want to apply for a separate long-stay visa and note the Schengen 90-out-of-180-day rule. While buying may be simple, the finances are anything but. Closing fees, including notary fees, typically add seven to ten percent to your purchase. Mortgage rates are also incredibly hard to secure for U.S.-based buyers, thanks to strict debt-to-income caps and FATCA compliance. To simplify the process, many buyers opt to put 20 percent to 40 percent down in cash.

Perhaps the most American-friendly market, Panama boasts a large English-speaking market, virtually no restrictions on foreign property ownership, and uses the U.S. dollar as legal tender in a vastly affordable market. The Central American nation also offers residency programs like the Friendly Nations Visa, clearing the way legally for residency by investment. In addition to an efficient legal system and reliable market, Panama boasts breathtaking beaches, lush rainforests, and exceptional wildlife.

If you’re hoping for a home in the coffee valleys of Colombia, you’ll need to hire a real estate lawyer before you start scouting for mountain chalets, coffee triangle treehouses, and rainforest retreats. The Caribbean country opts out of standardized agent licensing systems, making legal aid essential to verify title and freedom certificates. While citizenship isn’t required, you will need to secure a tourist visa and Colombian Tax ID to complete your purchase and register for property taxes.

The buying process in Greece is lengthy, though it is streamlined, with a clear checklist for American buyers to find success in the market. Hiring a local lawyer or notary is a great first step after scouting properties. Not only are they familiar with title checks and contracts, but they can also navigate the intricacies that come with buying in designated border areas or near military zones, which require special clearance for non–EU buyers. While the most popular areas for American buyers, like Athens and Crete, don’t come with heavy legal intricacies, buying any property in Greece will mean securing a Greek Tax Number—a small price to pay for cities with such rich histories and breathtaking seaside views.

The people in this popular Western European coastal country are famously friendly and inviting, and that extends to real estate. According to Samira Easton, a certified international property specialist with Premier Sotheby’s International Realty, Portugal offers a Golden Visa Program, which makes it simple to buy property and gain residency; clear real estate laws; and a well-established network of English-speaking real estate agents, lawyers, and notaries to help you during the process.

Belize is home to one of the most vibrant, tourist-friendly cultures in Central America, mixing Mayan, Hispanic, Asian, and Creole cultures in its dense rainforests and rolling low plains. Buying here is as easy as it can get, as outright ownership applies to foreigners, and a British-inspired legal system abolishes language barriers and makes contracts easier to understand. Even more, there is no capital gains tax in Belize, and property taxes trend low. The country also boasts vibrant marine life and is home to the world’s second-largest barrier reef.

Spain may have ended its investor visa program in 2025, but the real estate market is still remarkably easy for Americans to navigate. The country currently imposes no restrictions on foreign or nonresident property ownership, but there are a few things you’ll need to procure before you buy, like a Foreigner Identification Number, which is mandatory for taxes. Despite the minor hurdles, the market is booming both on the ground and online. “There are also numerous online resources and property portals available, so it’s easy to find listings and information,” Easton says. This is a boon for those who aren’t able to make the trip over to Europe to house shop in person.Note that buying property does not grant you residency or bypass the country’s 90-day Schengen limit. If you’re hoping to turn your Spanish holiday into your everyday, you’ll have to apply for a separate visa.

#6764. Sharna Burgess Calls Out ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Voting System for Letting Fans “Manipulate the Show”

Dancing With the Stars operates on a pretty simple scoring system that pretty much boils down to one part judges and one part audience. But pro Sharna Burgess thinks fans are using their online votes to manipulate the show and vote off talented dancers that shouldn’t be going home.

Speaking on Instagram Stories after Harry Shum Jr. and Jenna Johnson were eliminated, Sharna said (per E! News), “The online discourse is so wild that [it] was pretty clear who fans were ‘voting off’ and how they have felt about dance experience contestants.”

“[It’s] so disappointing to see our voting system is now being used by viewers to vote people off instead of voting for your favorite,” she added. “I say we got [sic] back to yall only have 10 votes total and you have to figure out how to dish them out. This voting ppl off thing is so insanely negative and not what we are built on and it’s seemingly given fans the chance to manipulate the show. JMO.”

There’s been a lot of discourse about the Dancing With the Stars voting system in light of Jenna Dewan and Harry Shum Jr. both going home despite delivering flawless routines and getting high judges’ scores. Jenna even alluded to the online discourse playing a role in her elimination, telling Extra, “I know it’s hard to know how this all plays into it. I know this was a particularly tough week for a lot of the online noise and all stuff that comes with the show. I don’t know, I’ll never know how, or if, any of that plays into this. But I know that we went out there and had the best time of our life.”

Dancing With the Stars producers haven’t addressed voting concerns or indicated that they’re making any changes, but stay tuned.

#6760. Survey Finds 84% of Organizations Reported at Least One AI-Related Incident in the Past Year

Companies are running into problems as they give artificial intelligence more control over critical business applications, with 84% reporting at least one AI-related incident in the past year, according to a new survey from identity security firm Delinea.

The company's 2026 Application Access Governance report found that some organizations have experienced incorrect financial transactions and AI actions they couldn't trace or explain. The findings raise concerns about whether enterprises have enough oversight of AI systems that can make decisions and take actions on their own

The findings are based on an independent survey of 1,000 senior finance and finance technology leaders in the United States and United Kingdom. The AI-specific analysis included 879 respondents whose organizations use the technology in critical business applications.

The report found that 35% of surveyed organizations have deployed AI agents capable of taking actions within business applications. However, only 11% of organizations using AI said their governance and security controls were keeping pace with deployment.

Delinea also found gaps in centralized visibility into agent deployment and permissions, including who owns agents and who approved their use.

Some respondents reported segregation-of-duties conflicts involving AI, while others identified agents that continued operating after the employees who deployed them had left the organization.

The research also highlighted gaps in monitoring access across business systems. Only 46% of respondents said they continuously monitor and manage cumulative access risks across applications.

Those gaps can make it difficult to identify segregation-of-duties conflicts, in which a person or agent has permissions across systems that should be kept separate.

Despite those issues, 95% of respondents said they were confident their access controls would withstand scrutiny in an external audit.

However, 26% reported access-related audit findings that had recurred in consecutive years, while 25% said some findings had remained unresolved for three or more years.

Only 22% of organizations had a single named owner for each application, with ownership tracked in one system of record.

Meanwhile, 60% reported an increase over the past two years in the number of critical applications operating outside the scope of access governance or Sarbanes-Oxley Act compliance.

The findings highlight the oversight challenges associated with giving AI agents authority to act within business systems, particularly when organizations cannot consistently identify who owns an agent or trace its actions.

Frank Vukovits, chief security scientist at Delinea, said organizations remain responsible for the actions AI agents take within their systems.

"When an agent can approve a transaction or change permissions, the business owns the outcome. You need to know what every agent can do, who owns it, and who approved it, before an auditor asks or your financials are misstated," Vukovits said.

For the full report, go to the Delinea site.

#6757. Report: Cyber Criminals Move Toward More Targeted Phishing Attacks

Cyber criminals are moving away from mass phishing scams and focusing on more targeted attacks, increasingly using trusted websites and services to trick victims, according to a new report from KnowBe4. The security firm found that spear-phishing attacks increased 41% over the past year.

The company's Phishing Threat Trends Report, Vol. 8 found that overall phishing volume increased 29.8% year over year. Organizations received an average of one dangerous e-mail for every 733 messages, but the bigger concern is how attackers are changing their approach.

Rather than sending the same fraudulent message to thousands of people, criminals are spending more time researching their targets and creating convincing messages that appear to come from trusted coworkers, businesses, and technology platforms.

The findings are based on threat data from KnowBe4 Defend, the company's inbound e-mail security platform, which processes approximately 1.15 billion event signals daily.

KnowBe4 found that several traditional phishing methods declined significantly compared with 2025. Mobile-focused scams dropped 47.3%, advance-fee fraud fell 31.3%, and missed-message scams declined 11.3%.

Meanwhile, spear phishing increased 41.1%, pre-attack reconnaissance rose 33.7%, and company impersonation climbed 28%.

The shift suggests that attackers are finding more value in carefully crafted scams that exploit existing business relationships and workplace routines.

Business e-mail compromise (BEC) remains particularly common, accounting for 51.2% of phishing e-mails analyzed in the report. Human resources, finance departments, and executive leadership are among the most frequently targeted groups.

Attackers also appear to be timing their campaigns around normal business activity. KnowBe4 found that 90.4% of phishing messages arrive Monday through Friday, with Monday accounting for the largest share at 20.6%.

One of the report's more troubling findings involves the growing use of legitimate technology platforms to deliver malicious content.

KnowBe4 found that phishing attacks were twice as likely to originate from legitimate infrastructure in the third quarter of 2026 compared with the previous quarter.

That includes AI services, where attackers are taking advantage of trusted domains to host fake developer consoles, login prompts, and credential-stealing pages. The report also identified a 228% increase in AI-brand impersonation attacks between March and August.

Because these malicious pages can operate on legitimate domains with valid security certificates, users may not see the usual warning signs associated with suspicious websites.

"For years, security advice has focused on a predictable checklist: Check the sender address, hover over the link, and look for the padlock," said Jack Chapman, senior vice president of threat intelligence at KnowBe4.

"While still foundational, that guidance is becoming less useful by the day."

The findings echo a recent report about management software and AI e-mail assistants. Research from Fortra and Barracuda showed how criminals are using legitimate administrative tools and hidden AI instructions to manipulate users and compromise systems.

A new leading role is on the horizon for Miss Piggy. Half a century after her on-screen debut, the Muppets’ most glamorous star is set to headline her first solo film, with Jennifer Lawrence, Emma Stone, and Cole Escola bringing the project to life.

The film is moving forward, at least on the page. During a Nov. 19, 2026 appearance on Watch What Happens Live with Andy Cohen, Lawrence offered a rare update on the project when a fan asked about its progress. “I don’t know, I always get in trouble for saying stuff,” she joked, before revealing, “We have a first draft!”

During a November 2025 appearance on Las Culturistas, Lawrence revealed that she and Stone are developing a Miss Piggy movie for Disney, with Oh, Mary! playwright and Tony Award winner Escola attached as the screenwriter. It marks the first collaboration between the two Oscar-winning actresses and the first time Miss Piggy will take center stage in her own feature.

“I don’t know if I can announce this, but I’m just going to,” Lawrence said on the podcast. “Emma Stone and I are producing a Miss Piggy movie and Cole is writing it.” When asked if she and Stone plan to appear on-screen, she added, “I think so. We have to.”

Plot details haven’t been shared yet, but the project is in early development at Disney, which owns the Muppets franchise.

While the storyline is still under wraps, the pairing of Escola’s wit with Miss Piggy’s flair for dramatics suggests a distinctly self-aware take on the beloved character could be coming.

Lawrence and Stone will produce the film, marking the first time the two have worked together professionally.

Since first appearing on-screen in The Muppet’s Wizard of Oz in 1974, Miss Piggy has been one of the franchise’s most recognizable characters, serving as an enduring symbol of self-confidence, ambition, and unapologetic glamour. Despite starring in multiple ensemble Muppet films, she’s never led a movie of her own until now.

The new project follows a recent wave of reimagined icons from Barbie to Wicked that has brought nostalgic characters back with modern points of view.

After a reporter told Lawrence that Grande was asked about the project earlier in the night, she responded that it was “the first time [Grande] was hearing” about a potential role. Lawrence added, “I guess it was one of those Twitter rumors. They’re good at those.”

#6752. Study: Sign-In Problems Are Primary Drivers of Help Desk Tickets

Sign-in problems account for up to 53% of help desk tickets at individual enterprises, but network issues can take significantly longer to resolve, according to a new study from ControlUp.

Analyzing 1,069,110 help desk tickets, the IT operations company identified password resets, account lockouts, and multifactor authentication (MFA) issues as major drivers of support demand.

At one large enterprise, account-related tickets took a median of 11 minutes to resolve, compared with 50 hours for network problems. Meanwhile, 87% of network and phone tickets were reassigned between teams, compared with 20% of account tickets.

MFA enrollment and re-enrollment problems appeared at 40% of organizations, with one in five linking these issues to employees replacing or upgrading their phones. Virtual desktop problems appeared at 55% of organizations analyzed.

Traditional phone support remained a major channel for IT assistance.

Among organizations that recorded how support requests were submitted, 57% reported that phone calls accounted for approximately two-thirds or more of contacts. At one organization, that figure reached 99%.

Machine-generated tickets from monitoring, security, backup, and patching systems appeared at half of the organizations studied. At 15%, automated tickets represented between 25% and 50% of all requests.

The research covered enterprises across retail, healthcare, financial services, manufacturing, construction, energy, and IT services.

ControlUp said the findings highlight the need to evaluate recurring problems and their operational impact rather than relying solely on ticket volume and resolution speed.

"IT organizations have traditionally measured support performance by how quickly they close tickets, but that doesn't tell the whole story," said Simon Townsend, senior vice president of marketing and office of the CTO at ControlUp.

"A password reset that takes a few minutes and an infrastructure failure that disrupts an entire location shouldn't carry the same operational weight."

For more information, go to the ControlUp site.

New research suggests that the value of AI should be measured not only by the work it eliminates, but also by the instructional insight and human connection it preserves.

AI-powered vulnerability research is contributing to a major increase in security updates, raising concerns that IT teams could fall behind as patching demands grow, according to cybersecurity company ThreatDown.

Sign-in problems account for up to 53% of help desk tickets at individual enterprises, but network issues can take significantly longer to resolve, according to a recent study from ControlUp.

A vendor coalition built on an Okta-developed blueprint is proposing a shared architecture for discovering, governing, monitoring, and containing enterprise AI agents.

#6751. Robert Pattinson and Suki Waterhouse’s Complete Relationship Timeline

Despite Robert Pattinson’s big-screen career and Suki Waterhouse’s supermodel status, the public doesn’t know much about their relationship. Both of these Brits prefer to keep their private lives out of the public sphere. As their romance continues past the five-year mark and the couple settles into parenthood, here’s a rundown of their secretive story.

Pattinson and Waterhouse were first linked on July 30 when E! obtained photos of the pair kissing in the streets of London. Per the outlet’s report, the two saw a 9 P.M. showing of Mamma Mia! Here We Go Again at Electric Cinema in Notting Hill. They then walked across the street for drinks at SoHo House until 1 A.M.An eyewitness said the couple was open with their affection. “Along the way, they were very close and cuddled and kissed many times,” the source told E! News. “Robert was very affectionate to her and had his arm around her the whole way and Suki reciprocated by holding his hand and kissing it. They were very loved up and close. Robert stroked Suki’s hair and gave her another loving kiss.”

An E! source said the pair was only “casually dating.” Since Pattinson had only recently ended a three-year relationship, “he isn’t looking for anything serious right now.”

The insider did express that the two “are definitely into each other” and are dating while Pattinson is in London filming The King. “Rob likes to have fun when he has breaks in-between filming and him and Suki have a great time together,” the source added.

LaineyGossip disputed breakup rumors that surfaced at the time, sharing that Pattinson had FaceTimed Waterhouse walking home from a London pub in August. Per the outlet’s report, the couple was spotted at Miguel’s concert in Los Angeles.

They each also attended the Toronto International Film Festival for their own projects that month. Pattinson was promoting High Life while Waterhouse walked the carpet for Assassination Nation.

The couple was photographed exiting London club Chiltern Firehouse at 2:15 A.M., seemingly confirming they were still together.

Photos surfaced of the couple leaving a Christmas party on London’s Regent Street. Waterhouse and Pattinson stayed at the party until the early morning hours.

Waterhouse and Pattinson spent her 27th birthday together, photographed leaving her party. A couple weeks later, People ran photos of them holding hands after a run in London.

Pattinson made his first public comments about his relationship with Waterhouse.

The Sunday Times asked Pattinson about Waterhouse, and he responded by asking, “Do I have to [talk about her]?” Then he went into more detail about why he’s so private about his personal life:

“If you let people in, it devalues what love is. If a stranger on the street asked you about your relationship, you’d think it extremely rude. If you put up a wall it ends up better. I can’t understand how someone can walk down the street holding hands, and it’s the same as when I do it and a hundred people are taking your photo. The line between when you’re performing and when you’re not will eventually get washed away, and you’ll go completely mad.”

However, he did tell the outlet that breakups with Kristen Stewart and FKA Twigs didn’t disillusion him to romance with a celebrity. “They were pretty long relationships, not like three months.”

When it came time for Pattinson’s 33rd birthday, he and Waterhouse were photographed at Chateau Marmont. Pattinson’s friend Tom Sturridge was also in attendance for the celebrations, per People.

During a June interview with Grazia, the reporter noted that Waterhouse FaceTimed Pattinson during a photo shoot break.

E! published a picture of Pattinson and Waterhouse strolling through London as they marked one year of dating.

In early September, photographer Sofia Malamute shared a photo of the couple cuddled together, captioned, “Ibiza, 2019.”

After several months of staying out of the public eye, Waterhouse and Pattinson confirmed their relationship was still going strong. The pair was seen leaving Waterhouse’s 27th birthday party in London on January 12, per LaineyGossip.

They celebrated her birthday at London’s Casa Cruz, and attendees included Liv Tyler, Georgia May Jagger, Cara Delevingne, and Ashley Benson.

Pattinson essentially confirmed that he was quarantining with Waterhouse during an interview for GQ’s June cover. According to the article, Pattinson had been sequestered “with his girlfriend” in the London apartment “the Batman folks rented for him” while production on the film was paused.

The couple signaled they were still going strong, two years into their relationship, with a pair of public outings. On July 21, Pattinson and Waterhouse were photographed walking arm-in-arm around London’s Notting Hill.

A source spoke to E! about the sighting, telling the outlet, “They weren’t recognized by anyone and they seemed to be in their own little world as they took a long stroll around the village chatting intently, pointing at properties and laughing together before they arrived at the club. They held hands the whole time or were linking arms and looked very happy.”

The following day, People published another photo of the couple on a walk. An insider told the outlet, “They are very loved-up,” adding, “they haven’t been together that long. But everything seems on the fast track. They are always laughing and smiling at each other.”

The pair’s most PDA-filled photo yet arrived about 12 days after Pattinson was reported to have tested positive for COVID-19, per CBS News. Although the studio never confirmed that report, production on The Batman was “temporarily paused.” It resumed in September, days after Pattinson and Waterhouse’s outing. In the photo, the couple sat on a London park bench with coffees, kissing while their masks were pulled down to their chins.

Pattinson’s recovery apparently only strengthened his relationship. A source told E! that “Rob and Suki have definitely discussed getting engaged but are in no rush. Suki is not pressuring him, and they are both very committed to each other.”

The insider confirmed the couple’s romance is “stronger than ever” after quarantine. “They love being low-key together and have very similar interests,” the source added. “They are truly inseparable and in love.”

Shortly after E! reported the two had discussed getting engaged, Pattinson and Waterhouse were photographed out with Pattinson’s parents in London. The two walked side-by-side behind Pattinson’s mom and dad on November 23. They opted not to show any PDA in front of them or the photographers who captured this outing.

“They are very solid in their relationship,” the source said. During the rare public appearances they do together, they’re “always just totally joined at the hip. You can tell he’s completely in love with her,” the source said.
